# Leads not yet added

Places someone read about while researching but did not add, with what is missing. Add to this list instead of guessing. When you add one to the Atlas, delete its line.

| Place | Where | What is known | What is missing |
|---|---|---|---|
| Harrower Pond Dam and the Pioneer Street mill site | Harrowers, Town of Amsterdam, NY (North Chuctanunda Creek) | John Garver (Union College) on Substack, 2019: a 25-foot masonry dam of about 1870, breached in the middle by local residents in May 1980 after the state found it unsafe; a limestone turbine room with a Swain turbine inside the west end; the 1871 Pioneer Street textile mill beside it demolished and the ground capped. He found the site gated, locked and posted, with a caretaker. A waterfall site puts a drop below an old dam at 42.96389, -74.16778 and calls it fenced and private | A public place to see it from; owner (private, unresolved in 2019) |
| Hagaman mill dam | Hagaman, NY (42.97583, -74.15139 per nnywaterfalls) | One waterfall site: a 10-foot dam beside an old mill building, seen from a parking lot on Mill Street north of the Pawling Street bridge | A second source for the mill and the dam, and who owns the lot |
| Chalmers Knitting Mills | Amsterdam, NY (42.93528, -74.19639) | Wikipedia: 1913 and 1916 underwear mill, on the National Register in 2010, demolished in 2011 with a Restore New York grant | Nothing known to be left; add only as `gone` if a series wants it |
| Schoharie Creek Preserve barns | Town of Florida or Glen, NY (Mohawk Hudson Land Conservancy) | MHLC: 198 acres given in 1998; two old barns and some farm equipment remain; 1.3-mile steep loop trail | Where the barns are and a coordinate; worth adding only beside something older |
| Enchanted Forest | Hope Valley, RI | Children's park, 1971–2005 | A source for what remains; a coordinate |
| Warren powerhouse ruins | Warren, RI | Named in passing | A source and a coordinate |
| Windswept ruins | Narragansett, RI | Named in passing | A source and a coordinate |
| Stillwater mill | Smithfield, RI (41.89444, -71.575 in Wikipedia) | A woolen mill of 1872 on the Woonasquatucket; 26 acres with the remaining buildings went to the Breakwater Preservation Conservancy in 2009. Third pass: the Smith-Appleby House (Historical Society of Smithfield) says the Stillwater Mill (Lister Mill) on Stillwater Road burned in May 1984, a housing development was built on the site in 2018-19, and a few stone buildings are the only parts left; it warns not to confuse it with the Stillwater Worsted (Levy) Mill in Greenville, now assisted living. The Wikipedia article mixes the two. The Stillwater Scenic Walkway (an old railroad bed from Capron Road to Farnum Pike) passes dams and remains of the village | Whether the surviving stone buildings can be seen from a public way, and which source is right about the mill's name |
| Grist mill and sawmill on the Ben Utter Trail | Arcadia Management Area, Exeter and West Greenwich, RI (trail at about 41.5996, -71.7490 in OpenStreetMap) | Hike New England (2007): just past the Austin Farm Road trailhead the left fork follows the earth dam of a grist mill, with 4-foot stone walls and a stone-lined race across the Falls River; a sawmill site with dry races lies off a short spur before the footbridge below Stepstone Falls | A second source with history, and a coordinate for either mill: neither showed clearly on the state LiDAR |
| Neutaconkanut Hill ski tows | Providence and Johnston, RI | NELSAP (from a 2004 reader report): three slopes above Plainfield Street and two rope-tow towers with their wheels; years unknown. Wikipedia's article on the hill and park (city park since 1903, National Register district in February 2026) does not mention skiing | Whether the towers still stand, a coordinate, and the years |
| Cranston Foundry ruins | Furnace Hill Brook, Cranston, RI (41.75548, -71.48760 in the Wikipedia list) | Wikipedia: remains of an iron foundry of 1812 up Furnace Hill Brook, inside the Furnace Hill Brook Historic and Archeological District (National Register 1980, address restricted) | Who owns the land and whether the public may walk there; the Register restricts the address, so think twice before pinning it |
| Big River Management Area: the reservoir that was never built | West Greenwich and Coventry, RI | Seen only as search excerpts (ecoRI, Providence Journal, the Water Resources Board's 2018 rules): the state took about 8,600 acres from 1964-66 for a reservoir, forcing 351 people out; the project was killed in 1990 and the land became open space in 1993. Of 200 buildings taken, 47 houses, 79 mobile homes and 3 commercial buildings still stood when the rules were written. A 2024 PAL report on Capwell Mill Pond dam ties the dams to the town's lumber industry. TNC's Harrington Preserve map (December 2025) marks cellar holes, a stone dam and two historic cemeteries on its land beside the management area | A specific cellar hole or mill site with a coordinate and a source that describes it; DEM's rules for walking there |
| Fort Church | Little Compton, RI (41.47944, -71.18333 in Wikipedia) | World War II coast artillery fort with 16-inch, 8-inch and 6-inch batteries on three reservations (one at the Sakonnet Golf Club). Wikipedia: all the batteries have been buried or built over | Nothing known to be visible; add only as `gone` if a series wants it |
| Gould Island | Narragansett Bay, RI (41.53357, -71.34444) | Navy torpedo-plane base from 1919; the state owns the south end as a bird sanctuary and the Navy the rest | What still stands, and the landing rules. Wikipedia gives neither |
| Conant Thread / Coats & Clark mills | Pawtucket and Central Falls, RI (41.88117, -71.39695) | A 50-acre thread-mill district; a fire on March 14, 2020 destroyed at least eight buildings | What stands now and who owns it |

Part of the land became the town forest, now part of Harvard Forest | A coordinate for the cellar holes and cemetery, and who owns that ground now |

The West Brookfield Historical Commission's historical-sites page (read November 2026) says the remains of the roads and two dams are prominent, and that marked public trails were still to be funded | eqlt.org and its brochure PDF answer 403 to page.py; a coordinate for the preserve, its parking and the mill site; what can be seen there |
